I can get irritated sometimes, but I haven’t really tracked it or thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I haven’t really paid attention to it in that way. Some tasks, maybe, but… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you have trouble sitting still or being quiet for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Sometimes I’m shifting around enough that people look over before I even notice I’m doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms of restlessness and difficulty focusing your entire life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, as far back as I can remember, it was kind of there. If my spouse is watching a show or someone’s resting, I’ll start humming or talking through what I’m doing, moving stuff around, and eventually someone asks me to settle down.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have mood swings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I wouldn’t really call them mood swings. I can get irritated sometimes, but I haven’t really tracked it or thought of it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. I haven’t really paid attention to it in that way. Some tasks, maybe, but… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: As a child, did you have trouble sitting still or being quiet for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My mother used to say I was “teaching the class from my seat” in elementary school. I was out of my chair a lot, talking, cutting in before the teacher even finished giving directions. So… it was there pretty early, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you often interrupted your spouse or others during conversations at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. More than I’d like to admit. At home I’ll jump in when my wife’s talking with someone, or even with relatives, and I think I’m helping clarify things. But I end up talking over them or kind of taking over before they’re finished.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're not in a meeting or a classroom?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do. Even in IEP meetings or my admin classes, my leg’s usually bouncing under the table, or I’m clicking a pen. Sometimes I’m shifting around enough that people look over before I even notice I’m doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced these symptoms of restlessness and difficulty focusing your entire life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, as far back as I can remember, it was kind of there. My mom used to joke that in elementary school I was “teaching the class from my seat” because I’d be out of my chair, talking, cutting in before the teacher finished. So it wasn’t something that just started recently or with the family stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have these symptoms caused problems for you at work or in your relationships?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it has. Work especially. I don’t mean to make things harder, but I think I’ve damaged some trust with colleagues and family, even when I’m trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Were you physically very active as a child, such as running, climbing, or having trouble staying still during play?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My mom used to say I was basically “teaching the class from my seat” in elementary school. I was always up, talking, cutting in before the teacher even finished giving directions. So… it was there pretty early, not just something that showed up later.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever lost a job or had significant problems in your relationships because of your difficulty with restlessness, impulsivity, or inattention?\n\nPatient: I haven’t lost a job, no. But it’s been… close enough to scare me. The complaints at work got serious, and I was put on a corrective plan.\n\nAt home, my wife says the caregiving routines are harder because everyone’s kind of managing my energy too. I don’t like admitting that. I think I’ve hurt some trust with people at work and with family, even when I’m trying to help.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any long-term friendships or relationships that have lasted without significant strain or difficulty due to your behavior?\n\nPatient: I mean… some relationships have lasted, yes. And at home, my wife has said the caregiving routines are harder because people are kind of managing my energy too.
